Read this first — honesty limits.

  • Maturity labels are plain-words labels from the universal research maturity scale (E1 Established, E2 Reproduced, E3 Engineering-ready, E4 Experimental, E5 Prototype, E6 Integrated, E7 Operational; T1 Theoretical, T2 Hypothetical, T3 Speculative; U Unknown). A label says how mature a technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.
  • Any dates, horizons, or timelines here are PLANNING WINDOWS, never predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs.
  • Public records are documented evidence, never established fact. Signature records are design-study, never presented as authentic public record.
  • No consciousness or sentience claim is made here. Machine consciousness is theories and testable models only, never an established fact; no claim is made that a machine is conscious, sentient, or aware.
  • Climate restoration records are research only; no proven-intervention claims are made.
  • Post-quantum records are defensive security only.
  • Synthetic biology is design-study only, with biosafety screening; no pathogen, toxin, gene-drive, or synthesis-enabling content appears here.

This website is the subject website for the Quantum Information Database database, public edition. It holds the full database: all 24 records.

All 24 record titles: What Quantum Information Is; The Quantum Bit; Superposition and Measurement; Entanglement; Quantum Gates and Circuits; Quantum Computing; Quantum Error Correction; Quantum Key Distribution; Quantum Networks; Quantum Sensing; Quantum Communication; Quantum Algorithms; Decoherence and Noise; Quantum Memory; Post-Quantum Cryptography; Quantum Standards and Testing; Quantum Simulation; Quantum Hardware: Superconducting Circuits; Quantum Hardware: Trapped Ions; Quantum Hardware: Light-Based Systems; Quantum Information and Security; Quantum Repeaters and Long Distances; Teaching Quantum Information; The Quantum Information Record Itself.

1. Descriptions

Planning windows: any dates, horizons, or timelines in these records are PLANNING WINDOWS, never predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs.

Terms in Plain Words

database: a database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to. record: a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. slug: a slug is the short web-address name of a database, written with lower-case words joined by hyphens. manifest: the manifest is the master list of all databases in the network, with each database's slug, record kind, identifier prefix, and record count. record kind: a record kind is the label that says what sort of entry a record is, for example a future advanced energy storage record. identifier prefix: an identifier prefix is the fixed letters at the start of every record identifier in one database, for example JAH-ADES-. generator: a generator is a program that creates new records or files on demand from a starting number called a seed, instead of storing every output in advance. seed: a seed is a starting number fed to a generator so that the same seed always produces the same output. generator gate: a generator gate is a check a generator must pass before its output is accepted, for example the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind. JCC code: a JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, is a short code that points to one exact record, written as JAHDB followed by the database slug, a hash sign, and the record identifier. API: an API, which stands for Application Programming Interface, is a defined way for one program to ask another program for data or work. MCP: an MCP, which stands for Model Context Protocol, is a plug that lets an AI (artificial intelligence) program read a database directly. AI: AI stands for artificial intelligence, which is a program that performs tasks that usually need human thought, such as answering, classifying, or generating. SQLite: SQLite is a small database engine that stores a whole database in one file on disk. SQL: SQL, which stands for Structured Query Language, is the language used to ask questions of a relational database. JSON: JSON, which stands for JavaScript Object Notation, is a plain-text format for storing structured data with named fields. HTML: HTML, which stands for HyperText Markup Language, is the language that marks up the structure of a web page. CSS: CSS, which stands for Cascading Style Sheets, is the language that describes how a web page looks. URL: a URL, which stands for Uniform Resource Locator, is the full web address of a page or file. HTTP: HTTP, which stands for HyperText Transfer Protocol, is the set of rules a browser uses to fetch a page from a web server. HTTPS: HTTPS, which stands for HyperText Transfer Protocol Secure, is HTTP sent over a protected connection. NIST: NIST stands for the National Institute of Standards and Technology, the United States measurement and standards agency that runs the post-quantum cryptography standardization project. QKD: QKD stands for Quantum Key Distribution, a way for two parties to share a secret key using quantum physics so that eavesdropping can be detected. PQC: PQC stands for Post-Quantum Cryptography, encryption designed to stay secure even against a future quantum computer. DOE: DOE stands for the Department of Energy, the United States government department for energy research and policy. test suite: a test suite is a collection of automated tests that check whether a program behaves as specified. demo: a demo, short for demonstration, is a small working program that runs and shows its result. website interface: a website interface is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses a system. JAH: JAH is the initials of Justin Addam Higgins, the maker name used across the network. design-study: a design-study is a Signature-made design, an invented proposal that is labeled as a design and never presented as an established fact. UNREACHABLE: UNREACHABLE is the label recorded for a resource whose fetch failed, together with the exact reason the fetch returned. maturity label: a maturity label is a short code saying how mature a technology is: E1 Established, E2 Reproduced, E3 Engineering-ready, E4 Experimental, E5 Prototype, E6 Integrated, E7 Operational, T1 Theoretical, T2 Hypothetical, T3 Speculative, and U Unknown. research question: a research question is the single clear question a research project sets out to answer, written before any work begins. prototype: a prototype is an early working model built to test whether an idea works in practice, before any finished product is made. simulation: a simulation is a computer model that imitates a real system so its behaviour can be tested without building it. validation: validation is the work of checking, by running tests and comparing against evidence, that a design does what its specification says it does. evidence label: an evidence label says what kind of evidence a record rests on: documented means built from fetched public records, and design-study means a Signature-made design. battery: a battery is a device that stores energy as chemical energy and gives it back as electricity when it is used. anode: an anode is the part of a battery where electric current flows out into the circuit when the battery is giving power. cathode: a cathode is the part of a battery where electric current flows in from the circuit when the battery is giving power. electrolyte: an electrolyte is the material inside a battery that lets charged particles move between the anode and the cathode while keeping the two apart. energy density: energy density is how much energy a storage device holds for its weight or its size, usually stated in watt-hours per kilogram. watt-hour: a watt-hour is a unit of energy: one watt of power used for one hour, and a kilowatt-hour is one thousand watt-hours. charge cycle: a charge cycle is one full charge and one full discharge of a battery, and a battery is rated for how many cycles it can survive before it wears out. round-trip efficiency: round-trip efficiency is the share of the energy put into a storage device that can be got back out again, stated as a percentage. grid: the grid is the network of wires, power stations, and substations that carries electricity from where it is made to where it is used. solid-state battery: a solid-state battery is a battery whose electrolyte is a solid material instead of a liquid, which can make it safer and able to hold more energy. flow battery: a flow battery is a battery that stores its energy in liquid chemicals held in tanks, so its capacity grows by using bigger tanks. supercapacitor: a supercapacitor is a storage device that holds electric charge directly on surfaces, charging and discharging very fast but holding less energy than a battery of the same size. lithium-ion: lithium-ion means a battery type that moves lithium ions, which are charged lithium atoms, through its electrolyte to store and release energy. state of charge: state of charge is how full a battery is, stated as a percentage from empty to full. thermal runaway: thermal runaway is a failure in which a battery heats itself faster than it can cool, which can lead to fire, and it is the main safety failure battery designs must prevent. photosynthesis: photosynthesis is the process green plants use to turn sunlight, water, and carbon dioxide into sugars and oxygen. artificial photosynthesis: artificial photosynthesis is a human-made system that copies the plant process, using sunlight to split water or to turn carbon dioxide into a fuel. photocatalyst: a photocatalyst is a material that speeds up a chemical change when light falls on it, without being used up itself. catalyst: a catalyst is a material that speeds up a chemical change without being used up itself. water splitting: water splitting is the chemical change that breaks water into hydrogen and oxygen using energy, here energy from sunlight. hydrogen: hydrogen is the lightest chemical element, a gas that can be burned or used in a fuel cell to release energy, leaving water behind. oxygen: oxygen is a gas in the air that living things breathe and that is released when water is split. carbon dioxide: carbon dioxide is a gas in the air, made when fuels burn, that artificial photosynthesis can turn into a fuel instead of letting it build up. solar fuel: a solar fuel is a fuel made using sunlight, such as hydrogen made by splitting water with sunlight, that can be stored and used later. photoelectrochemical cell: a photoelectrochemical cell is a device that uses light falling on a material in a liquid to drive a chemical change, such as splitting water. semiconductor: a semiconductor is a material that can absorb light and turn it into moving electric charges, and it is the light-catching part of most solar devices. light absorber: a light absorber is the part of a device that catches sunlight and turns it into electric charges that can do chemical work. electron: an electron is a tiny particle with a negative electric charge, and moving electrons are what electric current is made of. proton: a proton is a tiny particle with a positive electric charge found in the nucleus, which is the centre, of an atom. artificial leaf: an artificial leaf is a thin device shaped like a leaf that uses sunlight to split water into hydrogen and oxygen. qubit: a qubit, short for quantum bit, is the basic unit of quantum information, like a bit in an ordinary computer but able to hold a blend of zero and one at once. quantum bit: a quantum bit is the same as a qubit: the basic unit of quantum information, able to hold a blend of zero and one at once. superposition: superposition is the quantum property by which a qubit can hold a blend of zero and one at the same time until it is measured. entanglement: entanglement is the quantum property by which two qubits can be linked so that measuring one at once fixes the state of the other, however far apart they are. decoherence: decoherence is the loss of a quantum state when the qubit is disturbed by its surroundings, such as heat or stray electric fields. quantum error correction: quantum error correction is a set of methods that spread one qubit of information across many physical qubits so that errors can be found and fixed. quantum key distribution: quantum key distribution is a way for two parties to share a secret key using quantum physics so that anyone listening in can be detected. quantum network: a quantum network is a set of connected quantum devices that can share entanglement and send quantum information between distant places. quantum computer: a quantum computer is a computer that stores and processes information in qubits and uses quantum rules such as superposition and entanglement. quantum gate: a quantum gate is a basic operation that changes the state of one or more qubits, like a step in a quantum calculation. measurement: measurement is the act of reading a qubit, which forces its blended state to settle into a definite zero or one. post-quantum cryptography: post-quantum cryptography is encryption designed to stay secure even against a future quantum computer. quantum repeater: a quantum repeater is a device that extends a quantum network over long distances by refreshing entanglement partway along the path. quantum sensing: quantum sensing is measuring with quantum devices, such as using qubits to detect tiny magnetic fields or tiny changes in time.

What Quantum Information Is

JAH-QINF-000001 · quantum information identity · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Quantum Information Database, catalog item 29 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is What Quantum Information Is. Quantum information is information stored and processed using the rules of quantum physics, the physics of the very small. Where an ordinary computer stores bits that are zero or one, a quantum device stores qubits, which are quantum bits able to hold a blend of zero and one at once, and links them with entanglement, which joins two qubits so measuring one fixes the other. This record specifies quantum information science as one research subject: its units, its rules, its devices, its networks, and its honest limits. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact. The public edition records the research specification as documented public knowledge and established practice, with sources that were fetched and checked on 2026-11-17. The Signature edition holds Signature-made designs, each labeled design-study, which is a Signature-made design labeled as a design and never presented as an established fact. The research question this record answers is: How can information be stored and processed using the rules of quantum physics? The Quantum Bit

JAH-QINF-000002 · the quantum bit · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Quantum Information Database, catalog item 29 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is The Quantum Bit. A qubit, short for quantum bit, is the basic unit of quantum information, like a bit in an ordinary computer but able to hold a blend of zero and one at once. A qubit can be built from many physical things: a superconducting circuit cooled near absolute zero, a single trapped ion held by electric fields, or a single particle of light. This record specifies the research: what makes a good qubit, how its blend is written and read, and how long the blend survives before the surroundings disturb it. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The research question this record answers is: What makes a good qubit, and how long can its blend of zero and one survive? Superposition and Measurement

JAH-QINF-000003 · superposition and measurement · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Quantum Information Database, catalog item 29 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Superposition and Measurement. Superposition is the quantum property by which a qubit can hold a blend of zero and one at the same time until it is measured, and measurement is the act of reading a qubit, which forces its blended state to settle into a definite zero or one. The blend is where quantum power lives: a calculation can work on many blends at once. The cost is that reading the answer destroys the blend, so a quantum calculation must be designed so the wanted answer is the one most likely to be read. This record specifies the research: writing blends, protecting them, and reading them well. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact. The public edition records the research specification as documented public knowledge and established practice, with sources that were fetched and checked on 2026-11-17. The Signature edition holds Signature-made designs, each labeled design-study, which is a Signature-made design labeled as a design and never presented as an established fact. The research question this record answers is: How can blends of zero and one be written, protected, and read so the wanted answer is the likely one? Entanglement

JAH-QINF-000004 · entanglement · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Quantum Information Database, catalog item 29 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Entanglement. Entanglement is the quantum property by which two qubits can be linked so that measuring one at once fixes the state of the other, however far apart they are. Entanglement is the resource that makes quantum communication and much of quantum computing possible: it lets distant devices share a state no ordinary message could carry. This record specifies the research: making entangled pairs, keeping them entangled, measuring how strong the link is, and using it to send keys and to link distant quantum computers. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. Quantum Gates and Circuits

JAH-QINF-000005 · quantum gates and circuits · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Quantum Information Database, catalog item 29 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Quantum Gates and Circuits. A quantum gate is a basic operation that changes the state of one or more qubits, like a step in a quantum calculation, and a quantum circuit is a sequence of gates that carries out a whole calculation. Gates must be fast, accurate, and gentle: every gate adds a little error, and errors add up across a long circuit. This record specifies the research: gate designs for each qubit type, gate speed and accuracy, circuit design, and the counting that proves a circuit's total error stays below what the calculation can tolerate. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact. Quantum Computing

JAH-QINF-000006 · quantum computing · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Quantum Information Database, catalog item 29 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Quantum Computing. A quantum computer is a computer that stores and processes information in qubits and uses quantum rules such as superposition and entanglement. It does not replace ordinary computers: it excels at a few kinds of problems, such as simulating molecules, searching large spaces in a structured way, and factoring large numbers, while ordinary computers remain better at most everyday work. This record specifies the research: quantum hardware, quantum algorithms, the problems where quantum computers help, and the honest statement of what they cannot do. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. Quantum Error Correction

JAH-QINF-000007 · quantum error correction · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Quantum Information Database, catalog item 29 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Quantum Error Correction. Quantum error correction is a set of methods that spread one qubit of information across many physical qubits so that errors can be found and fixed. Qubits are fragile: heat, stray fields, and imperfect gates all introduce errors, and a calculation that runs long enough will be ruined without correction. The price is steep: one protected qubit can need hundreds or thousands of physical qubits. This record specifies the research: error-correcting codes, the threshold below which correction helps, and the counting that shows when a machine has enough good qubits to be useful. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact. The public edition records the research specification as documented public knowledge and established practice, with sources that were fetched and checked on 2026-11-17. The Signature edition holds Signature-made designs, each labeled design-study, which is a Signature-made design labeled as a design and never presented as an established fact. The research question this record answers is: How many physical qubits does one protected qubit need, and when is a machine big enough to be useful? Quantum Key Distribution

JAH-QINF-000008 · quantum key distribution · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Quantum Information Database, catalog item 29 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Quantum Key Distribution. Quantum key distribution, shortened to QKD, is a way for two parties to share a secret key using quantum physics so that anyone listening in can be detected. The key is carried on qubits, often on single particles of light, and any attempt to read them disturbs them in a way the two parties can notice. The key is then used with ordinary encryption to protect messages. This record specifies the research: key distribution protocols, the distance a key can travel, the rate at which keys can be made, and the tests that prove an eavesdropper would be caught. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact. The system identity is fixed: this is a future quantum information record, built as one database pair with a public edition and a Signature edition, each holding 24 records, so the pair holds 48 records in total. The public edition records the research specification as documented public knowledge and established practice, with sources that were fetched and checked on 2026-11-17. The research subject covered here is Quantum Networks. A quantum network is a set of connected quantum devices that can share entanglement and send quantum information between distant places. The research subject covered here is Quantum Sensing. Quantum sensing is measuring with quantum devices, such as using qubits to detect tiny magnetic fields or tiny changes in time. A qubit's blend is sensitive to its surroundings, and that sensitivity, which is a weakness for computing, is a strength for sensing: the smallest disturbance changes the blend in a way that can be read. Quantum communication is sending information using quantum states, most often carried on single particles of light through fibre or through free space. The research subject covered here is Quantum Algorithms. A quantum algorithm is a step-by-step method for solving a problem on a quantum computer, designed to use superposition and entanglement where they help. The famous examples search large spaces faster than ordinary methods and factor large numbers, which matters for encryption. Most problems gain nothing from quantum methods, and an honest algorithm record says so. JAH-QINF-000013 · decoherence and noise · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Quantum Information Database, catalog item 29 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Decoherence and Noise. Decoherence is the loss of a quantum state when the qubit is disturbed by its surroundings, such as heat or stray electric fields, and noise is the general name for all such disturbances. Decoherence is the central enemy of quantum information: it turns a useful blend into an ordinary random state. Fighting it needs colder devices, better shielding, quieter controls, and error correction. This record specifies the research: measuring coherence time, finding the sources of noise, and the engineering that pushes coherence longer. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The research subject covered here is Quantum Memory. Quantum memory is a device that holds a quantum state without measuring it, so the state can be used later. It is needed in quantum networks, where entanglement must wait for the far end to be ready, and in quantum computers, where states must be held while other parts calculate. JAH-QINF-000015 · post-quantum cryptography · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Post-Quantum Cryptography. Post-quantum cryptography, shortened to PQC, is encryption designed to stay secure even against a future quantum computer. A large enough quantum computer could break some of today's encryption, so new methods based on mathematical problems that quantum computers do not solve easily are being standardized by the National Institute of Standards and Technology, shortened to NIST. This is defensive security research: it protects ordinary systems, and it contains no instructions for attacking anyone. This record specifies the research: the new methods, their security, their speed, and the migration plan for moving systems onto them. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. The research subject covered here is Quantum Standards and Testing. Quantum standards and testing are the shared rules that prove a quantum device does what its makers claim: the qubits are counted, the gates are measured, the errors are counted, and the results are checked by a second laboratory. The research subject covered here is Quantum Simulation. Quantum simulation is using a quantum device to imitate another quantum system, most often a molecule or a material, so its behaviour can be studied without building it. The research subject covered here is Quantum Hardware: Superconducting Circuits. Superconducting circuits are one leading way to build qubits: tiny circuits of metal that, cooled near absolute zero, let current flow without resistance and behave as quantum objects. Their strengths are fast gates and manufacture with chip-making methods; their weaknesses are the extreme cold they need and the short coherence time of each qubit. This record specifies the research: circuit design, cooling, wiring hundreds of qubits, and the counting that shows how many good qubits a machine really has. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. The research subject covered here is Quantum Hardware: Light-Based Systems. Light-based quantum systems build qubits from single particles of light, which travel well, work at room temperature, and suit communication, but are hard to make interact, since light passes through light. Their strengths are natural links to quantum networks and no need for extreme cold; their weaknesses are the difficulty of making two photons interact for a gate. Quantum Information and Security

JAH-QINF-000021 · quantum information and security · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Quantum Information Database, catalog item 29 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Quantum Information and Security. Quantum information and security is the defensive study of how quantum technology changes what is safe: quantum key distribution can protect messages, quantum computers may one day threaten some encryption, and post-quantum cryptography defends against that threat. This record is defensive security research only: it protects systems and contains no instructions for attacking anyone. It specifies the research: threat assessment, defensive methods, migration plans, and the honest statement that no working attack on today's encryption by a quantum computer exists today. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact. Quantum Repeaters and Long Distances

JAH-QINF-000022 · quantum repeaters and long distances · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Description: This record is the long full official specification for one part of the Quantum Information Database, catalog item 29 of the 100 Future-Level Resources catalog. A database is an organized collection of records that can be searched, read, and added to, and a record is one complete entry in a database, with all of its fields filled in. The research subject covered here is Quantum Repeaters and Long Distances. A quantum repeater is a device that extends a quantum network over long distances by refreshing entanglement partway along the path. Light is lost in fibre, so a direct quantum link cannot span a continent; a repeater catches the entanglement, stores it in quantum memory, and passes it on refreshed. This record specifies the research: repeater designs, memory times, the number of repeaters a long link needs, and the staged growth from laboratory links to continental networks. The maturity label for this whole database is E3 Engineering-ready. E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact. The research question this record answers is: How many repeaters, with what memory times, can carry entanglement across a continent? 3. Science Proof

What Quantum Information Is

JAH-QINF-000001 · quantum information identity · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Science proof: The science proof rests on established practice and documented research, not on a new discovery. Organized knowledge, structured storage, indexed search, and automated testing are the standard methods of software engineering and information retrieval, which is the study of how to find the right records among many. The subject science itself — the physics, chemistry, and engineering behind What Quantum Information Is — is documented in the sources cited in this record, and this record states clearly where that science is settled and where it is still open research. A specification that names its parts, a database that stores its records in one shape, a search that returns records with their provenance, a generator that is seeded so its output is repeatable, and a test suite that checks behavior by running it are each established practice, and this system composes them without claiming a new law of nature. Nothing in this record claims a result that was not computed, and nothing here claims that a design is a finished deployed system. The maturity label E3 Engineering-ready is the honest statement of how far the subject has actually come.

Scientific equation: validation = executed demo + executed program + round-trip code + fetched sources + passed generator gates + stated maturity

Proof sketch: The proof sketch follows the validation chain: the demo is executed and its output is captured, the program is executed and reports a deterministic match, the JCC code is recomputed from the record and compared, every cited source web address is fetched and must answer HTTP 200, the maturity label is checked against the pair's label, and the generator is run with a fixed seed twice and must produce identical output. Each link in the chain is observed, not assumed, and a failure in any link fails the record. The scientific claim is stated, not machine-checked, and the machine-checked part is the arithmetic of the demo figures.

Proof status: The arithmetic is machine-checked by the demo; the scientific claim is stated, not machine-checked.

5. How It Works — Construction Workflow

What Quantum Information Is

JAH-QINF-000001 · quantum information identity · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Problem: Research question. How can information be stored and processed using the rules of quantum physics? The problem this research type solves is the gap between knowing a future technology by name and having a complete, testable specification for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a research question, a survey of existing knowledge with source references, a proposed innovation, a technical specification, mathematical and scientific foundations, a prototype or simulation plan, testing and validation criteria, risks and limitations, evidence and development status, and future expansion opportunities, the technology exists only as words. Ordinary information theory cannot describe what qubits do, so a science of quantum information is needed before quantum devices can be built or trusted. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. The horizons in the catalog are planning windows, not predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs, and this record never presents a planning window as a promised date.

Method: Complete specification, following the research standard in full. Existing knowledge and source references. The established background for this subject is documented in the sources cited in this record, each fetched and checked on 2026-11-17, and the record states which parts of the background are established science and which parts are open research. Proposed innovation. The innovation this record specifies is the complete, testable treatment of What Quantum Information Is as one research subject: one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder, so that future work can build on it without starting over. Technical specification. The research must satisfy every one of these requirements: the database covers quantum information science as one subject: qubits, entanglement, key distribution, error correction, and quantum networks; every record states its research question, its existing knowledge with sources, and its maturity label in plain words; devices are measured the same way everywhere: qubit count, gate fidelity, coherence time, and error rate; every term is defined in plain words on first use, with no unexplained jargon; retrieval works by identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search; records are verified by execution: demos run, programs run, and sources are fetched; the database teaches simple to compounded to most complex on a teaching ladder; expansion adds records in the same shape, with sources fetched and dated, never as bare names. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Qubit, which is a quantum bit, the basic unit of quantum information, able to hold a blend of zero and one; Bit, which is the basic unit of ordinary information, zero or one; Entanglement, which is the link between two qubits so measuring one fixes the other; Measurement, which is reading a qubit, which settles its blend into a definite zero or one. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future quantum information record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are qubits 50, gate fidelity per thousand 999, coherence ms 100, error rate per thousand 1, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14. Prototype or simulation plan. The prototype plan is staged: first a paper design with the figures stated and checked by the demo in this record; then a small laboratory model that tests one part at a time; then a simulation, which is a computer model that imitates the real system, that runs the same figures at larger scale; and only then a full prototype, which is an early working model built to test whether the idea works in practice. No stage is skipped, and no stage is called finished until its tests pass. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, the maturity label, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Expansion instructions. To expand this database, add records in the same shape, keep the identifiers sequential, keep the sources fetched and dated, and never present generated content as authentic public record.

Results reported: Testing and validation criteria. This record is validated by execution, not by assertion: its demo was executed at build time and its printed output was captured as the expected output, its program was executed and returned a deterministic match, its figures were recomputed twice with matching SHA-256 digests, and the whole Wave 1 Group B set passed CHECK 2, which is the second validation pass that re-checks every record, every demo, every program, every JCC code round-trip, every source web address, every maturity label, and every generator gate by execution. The acceptance tests for this research type are: the record is complete, with every required field filled; the demo executes and its output matches the expected output exactly; the program executes and reports a deterministic match; the JCC code round-trips, which means recomputing it from the record gives the same code; every cited source returns HTTP 200, which is the web server's answer that the page was found, when fetched; the generator passes its gates; the maturity label is present with its plain-words definition; and the record defines its terms in plain words. A record that fails any acceptance test is not filed as validated. Evidence and development status. The evidence label for this public record is documented, which means it is built from fetched public records, and its maturity label is E3 Engineering-ready. The demo figures for this record are qubits 50, gate fidelity per thousand 999, coherence ms 100, error rate per thousand 1, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14, and the demo prints them twice so that any change in the figures changes the printed output and the digests with it. Future expansion opportunities. The next work after this record is to deepen the neighbouring subjects it links to, to add records for the parts of the subject this record names but does not yet cover in full, and to re-fetch every source on a later date and record what changed, so the specification stays honest as the research moves.

When it applies: This record applies whenever the Master AI (artificial intelligence) needs to research, find, or check a future quantum information record: it gives the identity, the complete specification, the database structure, the search and retrieval rules, the working code, the generator, the website interface, the documentation shape, the demonstration, the automated tests, the validation results, the version history, the source provenance, the maturity label, and the expansion instructions in one place. It connects to the other catalog types as follows: Post-Quantum Computing Database (catalog item 30), which defends ordinary computers against future quantum computers; Photonic Computing Database (catalog item 32), which computes with light, including quantum light; Advanced Semiconductor Database (catalog item 31), whose chips carry the classical controls of quantum devices. The connection rule from the catalog is that one type feeds the next: a database can supply a website, the website can use a generator, and the generator can be registered in the generator database, so the Master AI can discover and reuse all three in future projects. The catalog's convergence rule also applies: one research breakthrough should be usable by other divisions, so a material, a method, or a measurement from this subject is recorded in a form other subjects can reuse.

Verification path: To verify this record, re-read the specification requirements in the method and check each one against the record; run the demo and confirm its output matches the expected output exactly and reports Deterministic: MATCH; run the program and confirm it reports a deterministic match; recompute the JCC code from the record and confirm it round-trips; fetch every cited source web address and confirm it answers HTTP 200; confirm the maturity label is E3 Engineering-ready with its plain-words definition; run the pair generator with seed 42 twice and confirm identical output; and confirm the terms glossary defines every term the record uses in plain words.

The Quantum Bit

JAH-QINF-000002 · the quantum bit · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Problem: Research question. What makes a good qubit, and how long can its blend of zero and one survive? The problem this research type solves is the gap between knowing a future technology by name and having a complete, testable specification for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a research question, a survey of existing knowledge with source references, a proposed innovation, a technical specification, mathematical and scientific foundations, a prototype or simulation plan, testing and validation criteria, risks and limitations, evidence and development status, and future expansion opportunities, the technology exists only as words. A computer built from qubits can do things no ordinary computer can, but only if its qubits can be written, held, and read reliably. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. The horizons in the catalog are planning windows, not predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs, and this record never presents a planning window as a promised date.

Method: Complete specification, following the research standard in full. Existing knowledge and source references. The established background for this subject is documented in the sources cited in this record, each fetched and checked on 2026-11-17, and the record states which parts of the background are established science and which parts are open research. Proposed innovation. The innovation this record specifies is the complete, testable treatment of The Quantum Bit as one research subject: one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder, so that future work can build on it without starting over. Technical specification. The research must satisfy every one of these requirements: the database covers quantum information science as one subject: qubits, entanglement, key distribution, error correction, and quantum networks; every record states its research question, its existing knowledge with sources, and its maturity label in plain words; devices are measured the same way everywhere: qubit count, gate fidelity, coherence time, and error rate; every term is defined in plain words on first use, with no unexplained jargon; retrieval works by identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search; records are verified by execution: demos run, programs run, and sources are fetched; the database teaches simple to compounded to most complex on a teaching ladder; expansion adds records in the same shape, with sources fetched and dated, never as bare names. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Qubit, which is a quantum bit, able to hold a blend of zero and one at once; Superposition, which is the blend of zero and one a qubit can hold until it is measured; Absolute zero, which is the coldest possible temperature, near which superconducting qubits work; Ion, which is an atom with an electric charge, used as a qubit when trapped. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future quantum information record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are qubits 100, gate fidelity per thousand 999, coherence ms 150, error rate per thousand 1, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14. Prototype or simulation plan. The prototype plan is staged: first a paper design with the figures stated and checked by the demo in this record; then a small laboratory model that tests one part at a time; then a simulation, which is a computer model that imitates the real system, that runs the same figures at larger scale; and only then a full prototype, which is an early working model built to test whether the idea works in practice. No stage is skipped, and no stage is called finished until its tests pass. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, the maturity label, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Expansion instructions. To expand this database, add records in the same shape, keep the identifiers sequential, keep the sources fetched and dated, and never present generated content as authentic public record.

Results reported: Testing and validation criteria. This record is validated by execution, not by assertion: its demo was executed at build time and its printed output was captured as the expected output, its program was executed and returned a deterministic match, its figures were recomputed twice with matching SHA-256 digests, and the whole Wave 1 Group B set passed CHECK 2, which is the second validation pass that re-checks every record, every demo, every program, every JCC code round-trip, every source web address, every maturity label, and every generator gate by execution. The acceptance tests for this research type are: the record is complete, with every required field filled; the demo executes and its output matches the expected output exactly; the program executes and reports a deterministic match; the JCC code round-trips, which means recomputing it from the record gives the same code; every cited source returns HTTP 200, which is the web server's answer that the page was found, when fetched; the generator passes its gates; the maturity label is present with its plain-words definition; and the record defines its terms in plain words. A record that fails any acceptance test is not filed as validated. Evidence and development status. The evidence label for this public record is documented, which means it is built from fetched public records, and its maturity label is E3 Engineering-ready. The demo figures for this record are qubits 100, gate fidelity per thousand 999, coherence ms 150, error rate per thousand 1, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14, and the demo prints them twice so that any change in the figures changes the printed output and the digests with it. Superposition and Measurement

JAH-QINF-000003 · superposition and measurement · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Problem: Research question. How can blends of zero and one be written, protected, and read so the wanted answer is the likely one? The problem this research type solves is the gap between knowing a future technology by name and having a complete, testable specification for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a research question, a survey of existing knowledge with source references, a proposed innovation, a technical specification, mathematical and scientific foundations, a prototype or simulation plan, testing and validation criteria, risks and limitations, evidence and development status, and future expansion opportunities, the technology exists only as words. A blend that cannot be read is useless, and a reading that destroys the blend must be designed around, not wished away. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. The horizons in the catalog are planning windows, not predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs, and this record never presents a planning window as a promised date.

The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Superposition, which is a qubit holding a blend of zero and one at the same time; Measurement, which is reading a qubit, which settles its blend into zero or one; Probability, which is how likely each possible answer is to be read; Blend, which is the mixture of zero and one a qubit holds before measurement. Quantum Gates and Circuits

JAH-QINF-000005 · quantum gates and circuits · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

The problem this research type solves is the gap between knowing a future technology by name and having a complete, testable specification for it. Quantum Computing

JAH-QINF-000006 · quantum computing · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Problem: Research question. Which problems do quantum computers genuinely help with, and which do they not? The problem this research type solves is the gap between knowing a future technology by name and having a complete, testable specification for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a research question, a survey of existing knowledge with source references, a proposed innovation, a technical specification, mathematical and scientific foundations, a prototype or simulation plan, testing and validation criteria, risks and limitations, evidence and development status, and future expansion opportunities, the technology exists only as words. A quantum computer is a specialist tool, and claiming it will replace ordinary computers misleads everyone who plans around it. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. The horizons in the catalog are planning windows, not predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs, and this record never presents a planning window as a promised date.

Method: Complete specification, following the research standard in full. Existing knowledge and source references. The established background for this subject is documented in the sources cited in this record, each fetched and checked on 2026-11-17, and the record states which parts of the background are established science and which parts are open research. Proposed innovation. The innovation this record specifies is the complete, testable treatment of Quantum Computing as one research subject: one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder, so that future work can build on it without starting over. Technical specification. The research must satisfy every one of these requirements: the database covers quantum information science as one subject: qubits, entanglement, key distribution, error correction, and quantum networks; every record states its research question, its existing knowledge with sources, and its maturity label in plain words; devices are measured the same way everywhere: qubit count, gate fidelity, coherence time, and error rate; every term is defined in plain words on first use, with no unexplained jargon; retrieval works by identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search; records are verified by execution: demos run, programs run, and sources are fetched; the database teaches simple to compounded to most complex on a teaching ladder; expansion adds records in the same shape, with sources fetched and dated, never as bare names. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Quantum computer, which is a computer that stores and processes information in qubits; Algorithm, which is a step-by-step method for solving a problem; Simulation, which is a computer model that imitates a real system, such as a molecule; Factoring, which is breaking a large number into the numbers that multiply to make it. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future quantum information record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are qubits 1000, gate fidelity per thousand 999, coherence ms 100, error rate per thousand 1, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14. Prototype or simulation plan. The prototype plan is staged: first a paper design with the figures stated and checked by the demo in this record; then a small laboratory model that tests one part at a time; then a simulation, which is a computer model that imitates the real system, that runs the same figures at larger scale; and only then a full prototype, which is an early working model built to test whether the idea works in practice. No stage is skipped, and no stage is called finished until its tests pass. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, the maturity label, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Expansion instructions. To expand this database, add records in the same shape, keep the identifiers sequential, keep the sources fetched and dated, and never present generated content as authentic public record.
