A name alone builds nothing: without a research question, a survey of existing knowledge with source references, a proposed innovation, a technical specification, mathematical and scientific foundations, a prototype or simulation plan, testing and validation criteria, risks and limitations, evidence and development status, and future expansion opportunities, the technology exists only as words. Two quantum devices alone are a curiosity, and a network of them is the infrastructure that makes quantum communication real. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. The horizons in the catalog are planning windows, not predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs, and this record never presents a planning window as a promised date.

Method: Complete specification, following the research standard in full. Existing knowledge and source references. The established background for this subject is documented in the sources cited in this record, each fetched and checked on 2026-11-17, and the record states which parts of the background are established science and which parts are open research. Proposed innovation. The innovation this record specifies is the complete, testable treatment of Quantum Networks as one research subject: one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder, so that future work can build on it without starting over. Technical specification. The research must satisfy every one of these requirements: the database covers quantum information science as one subject: qubits, entanglement, key distribution, error correction, and quantum networks; every record states its research question, its existing knowledge with sources, and its maturity label in plain words; devices are measured the same way everywhere: qubit count, gate fidelity, coherence time, and error rate; every term is defined in plain words on first use, with no unexplained jargon; retrieval works by identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search; records are verified by execution: demos run, programs run, and sources are fetched; the database teaches simple to compounded to most complex on a teaching ladder; expansion adds records in the same shape, with sources fetched and dated, never as bare names. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Quantum network, which is connected quantum devices that share entanglement over distance; Quantum repeater, which is a device that extends a quantum network by refreshing entanglement partway; Link, which is one quantum connection between two devices; Protocol, which is the agreed steps devices follow to share entanglement. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future quantum information record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are qubits 10, gate fidelity per thousand 999, coherence ms 100, error rate per thousand 1, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14. Prototype or simulation plan. The prototype plan is staged: first a paper design with the figures stated and checked by the demo in this record; then a small laboratory model that tests one part at a time; then a simulation, which is a computer model that imitates the real system, that runs the same figures at larger scale; and only then a full prototype, which is an early working model built to test whether the idea works in practice. No stage is skipped, and no stage is called finished until its tests pass. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, the maturity label, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Expansion instructions. To expand this database, add records in the same shape, keep the identifiers sequential, keep the sources fetched and dated, and never present generated content as authentic public record.

Results reported: Testing and validation criteria. This record is validated by execution, not by assertion: its demo was executed at build time and its printed output was captured as the expected output, its program was executed and returned a deterministic match, its figures were recomputed twice with matching SHA-256 digests, and the whole Wave 1 Group B set passed CHECK 2, which is the second validation pass that re-checks every record, every demo, every program, every JCC code round-trip, every source web address, every maturity label, and every generator gate by execution. The acceptance tests for this research type are: the record is complete, with every required field filled; the demo executes and its output matches the expected output exactly; the program executes and reports a deterministic match; the JCC code round-trips, which means recomputing it from the record gives the same code; every cited source returns HTTP 200, which is the web server's answer that the page was found, when fetched; the generator passes its gates; the maturity label is present with its plain-words definition; and the record defines its terms in plain words. A record that fails any acceptance test is not filed as validated. Evidence and development status. The evidence label for this public record is documented, which means it is built from fetched public records, and its maturity label is E3 Engineering-ready. The demo figures for this record are qubits 10, gate fidelity per thousand 999, coherence ms 100, error rate per thousand 1, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14, and the demo prints them twice so that any change in the figures changes the printed output and the digests with it. Future expansion opportunities. The next work after this record is to deepen the neighbouring subjects it links to, to add records for the parts of the subject this record names but does not yet cover in full, and to re-fetch every source on a later date and record what changed, so the specification stays honest as the research moves.

When it applies: This record applies whenever the Master AI (artificial intelligence) needs to research, find, or check a future quantum information record: it gives the identity, the complete specification, the database structure, the search and retrieval rules, the working code, the generator, the website interface, the documentation shape, the demonstration, the automated tests, the validation results, the version history, the source provenance, the maturity label, and the expansion instructions in one place. It connects to the other catalog types as follows: Post-Quantum Computing Database (catalog item 30), which defends ordinary computers against future quantum computers; Photonic Computing Database (catalog item 32), which computes with light, including quantum light; Advanced Semiconductor Database (catalog item 31), whose chips carry the classical controls of quantum devices. The connection rule from the catalog is that one type feeds the next: a database can supply a website, the website can use a generator, and the generator can be registered in the generator database, so the Master AI can discover and reuse all three in future projects. The catalog's convergence rule also applies: one research breakthrough should be usable by other divisions, so a material, a method, or a measurement from this subject is recorded in a form other subjects can reuse.

Decoherence and Noise

JAH-QINF-000013 · decoherence and noise · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Problem: Research question. What disturbs qubits most, and how can coherence be kept long enough to finish a calculation? The problem this research type solves is the gap between knowing a future technology by name and having a complete, testable specification for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a research question, a survey of existing knowledge with source references, a proposed innovation, a technical specification, mathematical and scientific foundations, a prototype or simulation plan, testing and validation criteria, risks and limitations, evidence and development status, and future expansion opportunities, the technology exists only as words. A qubit that loses its blend before the calculation finishes is useless, and its surroundings are always trying to disturb it. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. The horizons in the catalog are planning windows, not predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs, and this record never presents a planning window as a promised date.

Quantum Standards and Testing

JAH-QINF-000016 · quantum standards and testing · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Problem: Research question. Which shared tests prove a quantum device truly has the qubits and accuracy it claims? The problem this research type solves is the gap between knowing a future technology by name and having a complete, testable specification for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a research question, a survey of existing knowledge with source references, a proposed innovation, a technical specification, mathematical and scientific foundations, a prototype or simulation plan, testing and validation criteria, risks and limitations, evidence and development status, and future expansion opportunities, the technology exists only as words. Claims without shared tests are advertisements, and only a test that any laboratory can repeat can settle a claim. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. The horizons in the catalog are planning windows, not predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs, and this record never presents a planning window as a promised date.

Method: Complete specification, following the research standard in full. Existing knowledge and source references. The established background for this subject is documented in the sources cited in this record, each fetched and checked on 2026-11-17, and the record states which parts of the background are established science and which parts are open research. Proposed innovation. The innovation this record specifies is the complete, testable treatment of Quantum Standards and Testing as one research subject: one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder, so that future work can build on it without starting over. Technical specification. The research must satisfy every one of these requirements: the database covers quantum information science as one subject: qubits, entanglement, key distribution, error correction, and quantum networks; every record states its research question, its existing knowledge with sources, and its maturity label in plain words; devices are measured the same way everywhere: qubit count, gate fidelity, coherence time, and error rate; every term is defined in plain words on first use, with no unexplained jargon; retrieval works by identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search; records are verified by execution: demos run, programs run, and sources are fetched; the database teaches simple to compounded to most complex on a teaching ladder; expansion adds records in the same shape, with sources fetched and dated, never as bare names. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Standard, which is a shared written rule for how a thing is made or tested; Benchmark, which is a standard test that measures one thing about a device; Certification, which is the formal record that a device passed its tests; Repeatability, which is different laboratories getting the same result from the same test. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future quantum information record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are qubits 100, gate fidelity per thousand 999, coherence ms 100, error rate per thousand 1, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14. Prototype or simulation plan. The prototype plan is staged: first a paper design with the figures stated and checked by the demo in this record; then a small laboratory model that tests one part at a time; then a simulation, which is a computer model that imitates the real system, that runs the same figures at larger scale; and only then a full prototype, which is an early working model built to test whether the idea works in practice. No stage is skipped, and no stage is called finished until its tests pass. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, the maturity label, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Expansion instructions. To expand this database, add records in the same shape, keep the identifiers sequential, keep the sources fetched and dated, and never present generated content as authentic public record.

Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references. Demonstration. The demo in this record is the demonstration: it runs, it prints its results, and its output is captured as the expected output, never written by hand. Automated tests. The automated tests, which are a test suite, a collection of automated tests, check the demo, the program, the generator, the record kind, the slug form, the identifier prefix, the hard stop, the maturity label, and the JCC code, which stands for JAH Condensed Code, a short code that points to one exact record. Teaching Quantum Information

JAH-QINF-000023 · teaching quantum information · Maturity: E3 Engineering-ready — E3 means engineering-ready: the research has been shown to work in the laboratory and is ready for engineers to build with, but it is not yet a finished product in everyday use. The label says how mature the technology is; it never turns a guess into a fact.

Problem: Research question. How can quantum information be taught plainly, one idea at a time, to a reader with no physics background? The problem this research type solves is the gap between knowing a future technology by name and having a complete, testable specification for it. A name alone builds nothing: without a research question, a survey of existing knowledge with source references, a proposed innovation, a technical specification, mathematical and scientific foundations, a prototype or simulation plan, testing and validation criteria, risks and limitations, evidence and development status, and future expansion opportunities, the technology exists only as words. A subject that cannot be taught plainly cannot be checked, and a subject that cannot be checked cannot be trusted. The wider problem is duplication and drift: builders who cannot find what already exists rebuild it badly, contradict it, or miss it entirely, and systems that measure and identify things in different ways drift apart until their records no longer agree. This specification answers both problems by fixing one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder for the whole type, so that every later build of this type can be checked against the same standard. The horizons in the catalog are planning windows, not predictions or guaranteed breakthroughs, and this record never presents a planning window as a promised date.

Method: Complete specification, following the research standard in full. Existing knowledge and source references. The established background for this subject is documented in the sources cited in this record, each fetched and checked on 2026-11-17, and the record states which parts of the background are established science and which parts are open research. Proposed innovation. The innovation this record specifies is the complete, testable treatment of Teaching Quantum Information as one research subject: one identity, one structure, one retrieval rule, and one validation ladder, so that future work can build on it without starting over. Technical specification. The research must satisfy every one of these requirements: the database covers quantum information science as one subject: qubits, entanglement, key distribution, error correction, and quantum networks; every record states its research question, its existing knowledge with sources, and its maturity label in plain words; devices are measured the same way everywhere: qubit count, gate fidelity, coherence time, and error rate; every term is defined in plain words on first use, with no unexplained jargon; retrieval works by identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search; records are verified by execution: demos run, programs run, and sources are fetched; the database teaches simple to compounded to most complex on a teaching ladder; expansion adds records in the same shape, with sources fetched and dated, never as bare names. The system is built from these parts, each of which is specified in full: Teaching ladder, which is the ordering of records from simple to compounded to most complex; Plain words, which is explanations in everyday language, with every term defined; Worked example, which is one full example carried from start to finish; Unknown, which is what is still not known, stated honestly. Database structure. The database holds records of kind future quantum information record. Every record carries an identifier, a title, a subject, a description, a history, a problem, a method, reported results, a science proof, a math proof, a formal equation with a plain-words gloss, a scientific equation with a proof sketch, a skeptical analysis, an applications section, a verification path, a specification block with dimensions and care notes, key figures, a teaching-ladder rank, a dataset note, an evidence label, a maturity label, a demo with expected output, a working program, sources with web addresses and retrieval dates, network links, a generator block, and three scales. The checkable figures for this record are qubits 50, gate fidelity per thousand 999, coherence ms 100, error rate per thousand 1, records per edition 24, pair records 48, sources per record 3, sections 14. Prototype or simulation plan. The prototype plan is staged: first a paper design with the figures stated and checked by the demo in this record; then a small laboratory model that tests one part at a time; then a simulation, which is a computer model that imitates the real system, that runs the same figures at larger scale; and only then a full prototype, which is an early working model built to test whether the idea works in practice. No stage is skipped, and no stage is called finished until its tests pass. Search and retrieval. Retrieval works by exact identifier, by title, by subject, and by full-text search over every prose field, and every retrieved record returns with its sources and its provenance attached, so a retrieved fact can always be traced back to the page it came from. Working source code. The demo and the program in this record are real working Python code, not a description of code: the demo recomputes the record's own figures twice and compares SHA-256 digests, which are 256-bit fingerprints computed with the standard hashlib library, and the program wraps the same computation in a class with a run method, so the demo and the program agree by construction. Reusable generator. A seeded boundless generator, which is a program that creates new records on demand from a starting number called a seed, serves this database, and it must pass its generator gates, which are checks a generator must pass before its output is accepted: the emitted record kind must match the manifest record kind, the slug must use the single signature form, the identifier must use the database's own prefix, and the hard stop must be 1,000,000,000. Website interface. The website interface, which is the set of web pages through which a person or a program uses the system, gives one page per record with the full record, a search page, and an accessible version that is plain and readable without JavaScript, which is the programming language that runs in the browser. Documentation. The documentation explains the system in plain words first, defines every term it uses, and puts sources and citations last, so a reader meets the meaning before the references.
